## 12.0 Method, Status Rules and Limitations

### 12.0.1 Method

1. Source code first. Resource dictionaries were downloaded from WinUI 3 at a pinned commit (`f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34`, latest `main` on 2026-10-25). The files were 29 XAML resource files: 19 from CommonStyles, 9 from 8 control folders (InfoBar, ProgressRing, ProgressBar, NavigationView, Expander, TeachingTip, TitleBar, InfoBadge) and 1 from Acrylic. Four C++ material source files were also read. A deterministic parser (`winui/parse_tokens.py`) walked every `ResourceDictionary`, including the `Default` (= Dark), `Light` and `HighContrast` theme dictionaries. It wrote one row per resource with key, type, value, theme, file URL and commit. No LLM was involved, so these values are exact as of the commit.
2. Official documentation second. 52 Microsoft pages (Microsoft Learn design and developer docs, Win32 UX guide, DWM API reference, Fluent 2 site) were fetched in full. A structured extraction recorded rules, explicit numeric values, API names and resource keys, with an instruction to record only stated values.
3. Independent sources third. 20 queries that exclude Microsoft domains produced 158 search hits. 20 independent pages were fetched in full to corroborate or contradict. Search hits that were not fetched are registered as SNIPPET_ONLY and do not count as inspected.
4. Docs vs. code. Every documented value with a source-code counterpart was compared (§12.14). Disagreements are logged as GAP-B3-xx.

### 12.0.2 Evidence tiers and value tags

The tiers are the same as Part 1: T1 = Microsoft documentation, T2 = source code / official repository, T4 = independent article, T5 = forum. Tags used for values:

- **MEASURED (source)**: read from source code at the pinned commit.
- **DOCUMENTED**: stated on an official page.
- **ESTIMATE**: visual or derived. Every derived number is labelled as derived.

No pixel measurements of a live Windows 26H2 build were taken in this part. These are scheduled as VP-07 to VP-10.

### 12.0.3 Status rules applied

A WC leaf is COMPLETE only when (a) an official or source-code artefact fully enumerates the item, (b) docs and code were compared, and (c) open gaps do not affect the enumerated values. Otherwise it is PARTIAL. G items are marked PARTIAL (Windows evidence only), because the macOS and Linux evidence is still to come.

### 12.0.4 Limitations

- WinUI 3 resources describe the app framework. They are not proof of how Windows shell surfaces (Start, taskbar, Settings) are drawn. Shell surfaces may use internal or older styles. Per Rule 5, a WinUI token is therefore reported as a WinUI/Fluent developer design-system value, not an OS shell capability.
- Mica and Mica Alt parameters (tint, luminosity) are implemented inside `MicaController` in the Windows App SDK and inside DWM. Neither is part of the open WinUI repository. Only their documented behaviour is reported.
- The `SystemAccentColor` Light1–3/Dark1–3 shades are generated by Windows at runtime from the user's accent colour. Their algorithm is not published.
- Copyright: no Microsoft source files, icons, fonts or images are reproduced. Token values are cited as facts with file links. `fluent_tokens.csv` truncates style values at 400 characters and contains no template markup.

## 12.1 WC.01 Design principles — PARTIAL

| ID | Finding | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| WC.01.f01 | Microsoft's Windows app design overview frames Windows 11 design around its "signature experiences": color, typography, geometry, layering/elevation, materials, iconography and motion. Each has a dedicated page under `/design/signature-experiences/`. | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/); T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/design-principles) |
| WC.01.f02 | The Windows 11 design principles page is qualitative and written as first-person statements, e.g. the UI "fades into the background to help me stay calm and focused" and balances a refreshed look "with the familiarity of the Windows I already know". The extraction found 0 numeric values. | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/design-principles) |
| WC.01.f03 | Fluent 2 (fluent2.microsoft.design) is Microsoft's cross-platform design system site. It publishes its own design principles and a Windows component page that points back to WinUI. Fluent 2 web and React tokens are a separate token set (`colorNeutralForeground1`-style names) from WinUI's XAML resource keys. The two must not be merged. | T1 [Fluent 2 design principles](https://fluent2.microsoft.design/design-principles); T1 [Fluent 2 design tokens](https://fluent2.microsoft.design/design-tokens); T1 [Fluent 2 Windows components](https://fluent2.microsoft.design/components/windows) |

LitricOS note (ADAPT): use the signature-experience breakdown as a chapter skeleton for the LitricOS design language. Do not copy Microsoft's principle wording or names.

## 12.2 WC.02 Color and theming tokens — PARTIAL

### 12.2.1 Theme model (documented)

| ID | Rule | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| WC.02.f01 | XAML supports three themes: `Light`, `Dark` and `HighContrast`. Resources are looked up through `ThemeDictionaries`; the `Default` dictionary is used for Dark. | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/develop/platform/xaml/xaml-theme-resources); T2 [Common_themeresources_any.xaml](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Common_themeresources_any.xaml) |
| WC.02.f02 | Windows supports two colour modes (light and dark) chosen by the user. Removing `RequestedTheme` makes the app follow the user's setting, and custom templates should use theme brushes rather than hard-coded colour values. | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/signature-experiences/color); T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/develop/ui/theming) |
| WC.02.f03 | Accent colour comes from the user's Personalization > Colors choice. WinUI exposes it as `SystemAccentColor` plus `Light1–3` / `Dark1–3` shades. In Dark, `AccentFillColorDefaultBrush` uses `SystemAccentColorLight2`. In Light, it uses `SystemAccentColorDark1`. Its hover/pressed variants are the same colour at Opacity 0.9 / 0.8. | T2 [Common_themeresources_any.xaml lines 124–128 and 328–330](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Common_themeresources_any.xaml) |
| WC.02.f04 | Text contrast: at least 4.5:1 luminance contrast between text and background, aligned to WCAG 2.0 G18. `TextScaleFactor` ranges from 1 to 2.25. | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/accessibility/accessible-text-requirements) |
| WC.02.f05 | Microsoft's Fluent XAML Theme Editor exports `ColorPaletteResources` (Accent, AltHigh…BaseHigh, etc.). It flags colour pairs below 4.5:1. This is the legacy UWP-era palette API, separate from the Windows 11 `*FillColor*` tokens. | T2 [microsoft/fluent-xaml-theme-editor](https://github.com/microsoft/fluent-xaml-theme-editor) |
| WC.02.f06 | Independent catalogues list the 48-swatch Windows accent picker as hex values (for example Blue #0078D4, Red #C42B1C). This is a T4 transcription and is not verified against the OS. | T4 [abacktools.com](https://abacktools.com/tools/design/color-tools/windows-11-colors) |

### 12.2.2 High Contrast mechanics (MEASURED, source)

- WC.02.t-HC1: in the `HighContrast` dictionary, every one of the 83 core `Color` resources is set to `#FF0000` (83 of 83 rows). The `*Brush` resources in the same dictionary do not reference those colours. They bind to Windows contrast-theme system colours instead. The red value works as a sentinel that should never render, so a bug where a raw Color leaks into the UI would show up as red. (This is an interpretation of the pattern; no Microsoft statement was found. See GAP-B3-05.)
- WC.02.t-HC2: the HighContrast brushes across all parsed files reference eight system colours. The counts below are reference counts in the files parsed:

| System colour key | References |
|---|---|
| SystemColorWindowColor | 131 |
| SystemColorButtonTextColor | 106 |
| SystemColorHighlightColor | 86 |
| SystemColorButtonFaceColor | 80 |
| SystemColorWindowTextColor | 72 |
| SystemColorHighlightTextColor | 68 |
| SystemColorGrayTextColor | 58 |
| SystemColorHotlightColor | 4 |

This matches the guidance that apps must use system colour resources in contrast themes. Windows ships four contrast themes (Aquatic, Desert, Dusk, Night sky) with a contrast ratio typically at or above 7:1, and 2px borders are recommended on transitory surfaces ([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/accessibility/high-contrast-themes)).

Reading the table (derived, ESTIMATE): Dark text tiers are white at alpha 0xFF/0xC5/0x87/0x5D (≈100/77/53/36 %). Light text tiers are black at 0xE4/0x9E/0x72/0x5C (≈89/62/45/36 %). Control fills are low-alpha white overlays in Dark and semi-opaque white in Light. That means control fills are designed to sit on a material (Mica or Acrylic), not on a solid colour.

| WC.03.r02 | Segoe UI Variable has a weight axis `wght` from 100 to 700 (named instances Light 300, Semilight 350, Regular 400, Semibold 600, Bold 700) and an optical-size axis `opsz` covering 8–36 pt. | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/signature-experiences/typography) |
| WC.03.r03 | Minimum sizes are 14 px Semibold or 12 px Regular. The recommended line length is 50–60 characters; fewer than 20 or more than 60 is hard to read. | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/signature-experiences/typography) |
| WC.03.r04 | On the web, Segoe UI Variable is exposed as three families (Small, Text, Display) with an `opsz` of 1 / 10.5 / 36 and weights 300–700. This is an independent technique, not an official API. | T4 [tigeroakes.com](https://tigeroakes.com/posts/segoe-ui-variable/) |
| WC.03.r05 | The duplicate legacy page `/design/style/typography` (dated 2024-11-05) carries the same ramp. The current canonical page is under `signature-experiences`. | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/style/typography) |

The line heights are not encoded in the WinUI resources. `LineStackingStrategy=MaxHeight` lets the font's own metrics decide. A LitricOS implementation that needs the documented 16/20/24/28/36/52/92 must set them explicitly (GAP-B3-04).

Note (ADOPT): the Thickness values are frequently asymmetric, for example `ButtonPadding` 11,5,11,6 and `MenuFlyoutItemThemePadding` 11,8,11,9. The extra bottom pixel compensates for Segoe UI's baseline so text looks optically centred. LitricOS should test optical centring with its own font instead of copying the numbers.

The blur radius, offset and opacity that a given depth produces are not published (GAP-B3-08). A visual measurement on a live build (VP-08) is required before LitricOS fixes any shadow recipe.

## 12.7 WC.07 Materials: Mica, Mica Alt, Acrylic, Smoke — PARTIAL

### 12.7.1 Documented behaviour

| ID | Rule | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| WC.07.r01 | Mica is an opaque material for long-lived base layers (the app window background). It samples the desktop wallpaper once, not continuously, and tints it with the theme colour. Mica Alt has stronger tinting of the user's desktop background colour and is recommended when the title bar needs contrast against commanding areas (navigation, menus). When transparency is off (Settings > Personalization > Color), Mica falls back to `SolidBackgroundFillColorBase` and Mica Alt to `SolidBackgroundFillColorBaseAlt`. In High Contrast the user's chosen background colour replaces both. | T1 [Mica material (2026-07-22)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/style/mica) |
| WC.07.r02 | Mica is Windows 11-only; on Windows 10 it falls back to a solid theme colour. Mica and Desktop Acrylic need DirectX 11 and enough GPU memory. Desktop Acrylic is available from Windows 10 build 17763. | T1 [Materials (2026-08-19)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/develop/ui/materials) |
| WC.07.r03 | Acrylic is a translucent, blurred material for transient, light-dismiss surfaces (flyouts, context menus). There are two kinds. Background (desktop) acrylic samples what is behind the window. In-app acrylic samples content inside the app. Avoid layering multiple acrylic surfaces. Background acrylic is recommended for context menus, flyouts, non-modal popups and light-dismiss panes. | T1 [Acrylic material (2026-10-02)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/style/acrylic) |
| WC.07.r04 | WinUI 3 apps set `Window.SystemBackdrop` to `MicaBackdrop` (property `Kind` = Base / BaseAlt) or `DesktopAcrylicBackdrop` (Windows App SDK 1.3+). For per-element backdrops, `SystemBackdropElement` requires Windows App SDK 2.0. Custom controllers expose four parameters: `FallbackColor`, `LuminosityOpacity`, `TintColor` and `TintOpacity`. | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/develop/ui/system-backdrops); T2 [MicaBackdrop.cpp](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/Materials/MicaBackdrop/MicaBackdrop.cpp) |
| WC.07.r05 | Win32 apps use `DwmSetWindowAttribute(DWMWA_SYSTEMBACKDROP_TYPE = 38)`, supported from Windows 11 build 22621. Older `DWMWA_USE_HOSTBACKDROPBRUSH` and `DWMWA_USE_IMMERSIVE_DARK_MODE` (20) are supported from build 22000. | T1 [DWMWINDOWATTRIBUTE (2026-03-09)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/dwmapi/ne-dwmapi-dwmwindowattribute); T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/desktop/modernize/ui/apply-mica-win32); T4 [comcomponent.com (JA)](https://comcomponent.com/knowledge/dwm-system-backdrop/) |
| WC.07.r06 | Smoke: `SmokeFillColorDefault` = `#4D000000` (≈30 % black) in both Light and Dark. It is the scrim behind modal ContentDialogs. | T2 §12.2.3 |

### 12.7.2 Acrylic recipe (MEASURED, source)

From [AcrylicBrush.h](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/Materials/Acrylic/AcrylicBrush.h) and [AcrylicBrush.cpp](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/Materials/Acrylic/AcrylicBrush.cpp):

| ID | Constant | Value |
|---|---|---|
| WC.07.t01 | `sc_blurRadius` (Gaussian blur, `EffectBorderMode::Hard`) | 30.0 |
| WC.07.t02 | `sc_noiseOpacity` (noise texture layer) | 0.02 |
| WC.07.t03 | `sc_saturation` | 1.25 |
| WC.07.t04 | Tint opacity ceiling by tint luminosity: white | 0.45 |
| WC.07.t05 | Ceiling at 50 % luminosity (gray) | 0.90 |
| WC.07.t06 | Ceiling for black | 0.85 |
| WC.07.t07 | Fallback switch / tint transition duration | 167 ms (`switchDuration = 167ms`) |

The effect order in the code comments is: backdrop → saturation → Gaussian blur → luminosity blend (with luminosity colour) → colour blend with tint → noise. The tint ceiling is suppressed further when saturation is low, according to the comment at lines 211–215 of AcrylicBrush.cpp. When `TintLuminosityOpacity` is set explicitly, the code skips the suppression ("we don't intervene into users parameters").

LitricOS note (ADAPT): this layer order is a sound generic recipe (blur + luminosity + tint + noise) for a compositor-side material. Implement it independently. The values above are reference points for visual parity tests, not values to copy.

## 12.8 WC.08 Iconography — PARTIAL

| ID | Finding | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| WC.08.r01 | Segoe Fluent Icons is the Windows 11 system icon font, version 1.56. 170 icons have been added since 1.00. Glyphs are designed for 16, 20, 24, 32, 40, 48 and 64 px. Code points E0xx–E5xx are legacy and deprecated. | T1 [Segoe Fluent Icons font (2026-10-14)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/iconography/segoe-fluent-icons-font) |
| WC.08.r02 | The official glyph table, counted from the page HTML on 2026-10-25, has 1,744 table rows and 1,574 unique code points from E700 to F8CC (MEASURED by regex count; some glyphs appear in more than one table). | same |
| WC.08.r03 | WinUI resolves the icon font through `SymbolThemeFontFamily`. On Windows 10 20H2 and earlier, Segoe Fluent Icons is unavailable and Segoe MDL2 Assets is used instead. Icon elements are `FontIcon`, `SymbolIcon`, `PathIcon`, `BitmapIcon`, `ImageIcon` and `AnimatedIcon`. Each has an `*IconSource` equivalent, and only IconSource can be shared from a ResourceDictionary. | T1 [Icons in Windows apps (2024-07-29)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/style/icons) |
| WC.08.r04 | Fluent UI System Icons is a separate MIT-licensed SVG/font set (repo `microsoft/fluentui-system-icons`, packages at v1.1.344). It ships Regular, Filled and Color styles and `directionType` metadata (`unique` / `mirror`) for RTL. It is not the same as Segoe Fluent Icons. | T2 [microsoft/fluentui-system-icons](https://github.com/microsoft/fluentui-system-icons); T1 [Fluent 2 iconography](https://fluent2.microsoft.design/iconography) |
| WC.08.r05 | Icon layering: glyphs designed to be stacked (for example outline + fill) can be overlaid, and a set of glyphs mirror in RTL. | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/iconography/segoe-fluent-icons-font) |
| WC.08.r06 | Independent catalogue: 1,533 glyphs; 145 have a same-name Fluent UI System Icon. | T4 [fluenticons.co](https://fluenticons.co/segoe-fluent-icons/) |

Licence warning (AVOID): the Segoe fonts are licensed for Windows and cannot be redistributed with LitricOS. Fluent UI System Icons is MIT-licensed, but it carries Microsoft's visual identity. Per brief Rule 10, LitricOS should commission its own icon set and borrow only structural ideas: size grid, layering, mirroring metadata.

| WC.09.t06 | ScrollBar expand / contract | `00:00:00.167` (WC.T2831) / `00:00:00.167` (WC.T2834) | MEASURED | T2 [ScrollBar_themeresources.xaml](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/CommonStyles/ScrollBar_themeresources.xaml) |
| WC.09.t07 | ScrollBar opacity / colour change | `00:00:00.083` (WC.T2832) / `00:00:00.083` (WC.T2833) | MEASURED | same |
| WC.09.t08 | Button `BrushTransition` | 0:0:0.083 (3 occurrences) | MEASURED | T2 [Button_themeresources.xaml](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Button_themeresources.xaml) |
| WC.09.d01 | Fast Out, Slow In (entering) | cubic-bezier(0, 0, 0, 1) | DOCUMENTED | T1 [Timing and easing (2026-02-28)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/motion/timing-and-easing) |
| WC.09.d02 | Slow Out, Fast In (exiting) | cubic-bezier(1, 0, 1, 1) | DOCUMENTED | same |
| WC.09.d03 | Direct Entrance | (0,0,0,1); 167 / 250 / 333 ms | DOCUMENTED | T1 [Motion (2026-02-28)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/signature-experiences/motion) |
| WC.09.d04 | Existing elements (point to point) | (0.55,0.55,0,1); 167 / 250 / 333 ms | DOCUMENTED | same |
| WC.09.d05 | Direct Exit | (0,0,0,1); 167 ms; always combined with a fade-out | DOCUMENTED | same |
| WC.09.d06 | Gentle Exit | (1,0,1,1); 167 ms | DOCUMENTED | same |
| WC.09.d07 | Bare minimum (opacity fade) | Linear; 83 ms | DOCUMENTED | same |
| WC.09.d08 | Strong Entrance (elastic in, 3 keyframes) | (0.85,0,0,1) 167 ms → (0.85,0,0.75,1) 167 ms → (0.85,0,0,1) 333 ms | DOCUMENTED | same |
| WC.09.d09 | Legacy page-transition examples | slide 150 px / 300 ms, fade 150 ms, grow 300/150 ms | DOCUMENTED (2024 page) | T1 [Motion in practice (2024-11-05)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/motion/motion-in-practice) |

The tokens form a 83 / 167 / 250 / 333 ms ladder. Each step is about 1/12 s (≈ 5 / 10 / 15 / 20 frames at 60 Hz, derived), which suggests the ladder was designed in frames. Contradictions are logged in §12.14: GAP-B3-02 (Direct Exit curve) and GAP-B3-03 (legacy 300/150 ms durations). Reduced motion (Settings > Accessibility > Visual effects > Animation effects) is covered in Part 2 (WB). WinUI checks it through `UISettings.AnimationsEnabled`, but this was not re-verified in this part.

## 12.10 WC.10 Control states — PARTIAL

### 12.10.1 Button state map (MEASURED, source)

Source: [Button_themeresources.xaml](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Button_themeresources.xaml). Cells name the resolved brush key.

| State | Background (Dark/Light) | Foreground | Border | HighContrast background |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rest | `ControlFillColorDefaultBrush` | `TextFillColorPrimaryBrush` | `ControlElevationBorderBrush` | `SystemControlBackgroundBaseLowBrush` |
| PointerOver | `ControlFillColorSecondaryBrush` | `TextFillColorPrimaryBrush` | `ControlElevationBorderBrush` | `SystemColorHighlightTextColorBrush` |
| Pressed | `ControlFillColorTertiaryBrush` | `TextFillColorSecondaryBrush` | `ControlStrokeColorDefaultBrush` | `SystemColorHighlightTextColorBrush` |
| Disabled | `ControlFillColorDisabledBrush` | `TextFillColorDisabledBrush` | `ControlStrokeColorDefaultBrush` | `SystemControlBackgroundBaseLowBrush` |
| Accent rest | `AccentFillColorDefaultBrush` | `TextOnAccentFillColorPrimaryBrush` | `AccentControlElevationBorderBrush` | `SystemControlHighlightAccentBrush` |
| Accent hover | `AccentFillColorSecondaryBrush` | `TextOnAccentFillColorPrimaryBrush` | `AccentControlElevationBorderBrush` | `SystemControlForegroundAccentBrush` |
| Accent pressed | `AccentFillColorTertiaryBrush` | `TextOnAccentFillColorSecondaryBrush` | `ControlFillColorTransparentBrush` | `SystemControlBackgroundBaseMediumLowBrush` |
| Accent disabled | `AccentFillColorDisabledBrush` | `TextOnAccentFillColorDisabled` | `ControlFillColorTransparentBrush` | `SystemControlBackgroundBaseLowBrush` |

Pattern (ADOPT as a concept): Dark and Light share brush keys. Only the underlying colour values differ (§12.2.3). Rest → hover → pressed steps through Default → Secondary → Tertiary fill. Pressed also dims the foreground to `TextFillColorSecondary`. The rest border is an elevation gradient brush (`ControlElevationBorderBrush`) that is flattened when pressed. Brush changes animate over 83 ms (`BrushTransition`).

### 12.10.2 Other state rules

| ID | Rule | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| WC.10.r01 | Visual feedback depends on the input device (touch, touchpad, mouse, pen, keyboard). Contact visualizations are critical for touch interactions that need precision. Touch visualizations should not be used where they would interfere with the app. | T1 [Visual feedback (2026-09-27)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/develop/input/guidelines-for-visualfeedback) |
| WC.10.r02 | Buttons: at least 120 px wide for short labels, and labels no longer than 26 characters. Default `ClickMode` is `Release`. `RepeatButton` defaults are Delay 500 ms and Interval 100 ms. A SplitButton flyout opens `BottomEdgeAlignedLeft`. | T1 [Buttons (2026-02-28)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/controls/buttons) |
| WC.10.r03 | The default `Button` style sets no `MinWidth`. The 120 px minimum is guidance only, and the developer must enforce it (GAP-B3-09). | T2 [Button_themeresources.xaml](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Button_themeresources.xaml) |
| WC.10.r04 | TextBox focus: the bottom border grows from 1 to 2 px (`TextControlBorderThemeThicknessFocused` = 1,1,1,2). In HighContrast, all sides become 2 px. | T2 [Common_themeresources.xaml](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Common_themeresources.xaml) |
| WC.10.r05 | `Button_themeresources_perf2026.xaml` and `Expander_themeresources_perf2026.xaml` exist at this commit. In the Button variant, the `Storyboard`/`ObjectAnimationUsingKeyFrames` state changes are replaced with `VisualState.Setters`, and every resource key and value is identical. The purpose ("performance 2026") is inferred from the name only. It is undocumented (GAP-B3-06). | T2 diff of the two files at commit `f980ab26e3` |

## 12.11 WC.11 Focus visuals — PARTIAL

| ID | Rule / value | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| WC.11.r01 | High-visibility focus visual: a 2 px primary (outer) border and a 1 px secondary (inner) border, offset 1 px outside the control bounds by default. Properties: `FocusVisualPrimaryThickness`, `FocusVisualSecondaryThickness`, `FocusVisualMargin`, `FocusVisualPrimaryBrush`, `FocusVisualSecondaryBrush`. | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/develop/input/guidelines-for-visualfeedback) |
| WC.11.r02 | Colours: outer `FocusStrokeColorOuter` (Dark `#FFFFFF`, Light `#E4000000`), inner `FocusStrokeColorInner` (`#B3000000` / `#B3FFFFFF`). In HighContrast the outer brush is `SystemColorWindowTextColor`. `UseSystemFocusVisuals` = True by default. | T2 §12.2.3; [Common_themeresources.xaml](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Common_themeresources.xaml) |
| WC.11.r03 | Per-control overrides: CheckBox `FocusVisualMargin` -7,-3,-7,-3; Slider -6,-14,-6,-14 (horizontal) and -14,-6,-14,-6 (vertical). | T2 [CheckBox_themeresources.xaml](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/CommonStyles/CheckBox_themeresources.xaml); [Slider_themeresources.xaml](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/CommonStyles/Slider_themeresources.xaml) |
| WC.11.r04 | Keyboard: Tab / Shift+Tab move focus in `TabIndex` order (an unset `TabIndex` = Int32.MaxValue). F6 / Shift+F6 cycle between major panes. Arrow keys move within composite controls. Access keys (Alt) and accelerators should be provided. | T1 [Keyboard accessibility (2026-10-04)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/accessibility/keyboard-accessibility); T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/develop/input/keyboard-interactions) |
| WC.11.r05 | Reveal Focus is an optional animated border glow for 10-foot (Xbox/TV) experiences. Whether system focus visuals are suppressed for pointer input was not stated on the pages read (test in Part 13). | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/develop/input/guidelines-for-visualfeedback) |

## 12.12 WC.12 Win32 and common controls conventions — PARTIAL

| ID | Finding | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| WC.12.r01 | The Windows UX Interaction Guidelines (Win32 "uxguide") remain online as the legacy desktop style guide. They cover controls, commands, text, windows and interaction, and include a "top violations" checklist. They predate Windows 11 and use Windows 7-era terminology, so they are not current Fluent guidance. | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/uxguide/how-to-design-desktop-ux); [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/uxguide/controls); [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/uxguide/interaction); [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/uxguide/top-violations) |
| WC.12.r02 | Visual styles require ComCtl32.dll version 6, declared in the application manifest. Since Windows 8, visual styles are always on. | T1 [Visual styles overview (2020-11-04)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/controls/visual-styles-overview) |
| WC.12.r03 | Dark title bars for Win32 use `DWMWA_USE_IMMERSIVE_DARK_MODE` (20). Apps detect the theme through `UISettings` colour values (foreground perceived-brightness test). The page covers the title bar and app surface ("the entirety of an app's surface must follow the dark theme"). It documents no API that switches the Win32 common controls themselves to dark. | T1 [Support dark and light themes in Win32 apps (2026-06-16)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/desktop/modernize/ui/apply-windows-themes) |
| WC.12.r04 | Because there is no public dark-mode API for common controls, third parties rely on undocumented uxtheme ordinals or custom drawing. An example is win32-darkmodelib (MPL 2.0, used by 7-Zip), supporting Windows 10 1809+ and Windows 11. Treat this as an undocumented behaviour risk (AVOID relying on it). | T2 [ozone10/win32-darkmodelib](https://github.com/ozone10/win32-darkmodelib); T4 [Xojo blog (2021)](https://blog.xojo.com/2021/11/18/welcome-to-the-dark-side-of-windows/) |
| WC.12.r05 | Border colour, caption colour and text colour can be set per window (`DWMWA_BORDER_COLOR` etc.). `DWMWA_COLOR_NONE` (0xFFFFFFFE) suppresses the border, and `DWMWA_COLOR_DEFAULT` (0xFFFFFFFF) resets it. Build 26100 adds `DWMWA_REDIRECTIONBITMAP_ALPHA` and `DWMWA_BORDER_MARGINS`. | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/dwmapi/ne-dwmapi-dwmwindowattribute) |

Result: Windows 11 runs three visual generations at once (WinUI/Fluent, Win32 visual styles, legacy uxguide-era dialogs; see Part 3 GAP-B2 rows on Control Panel). LitricOS should ship one toolkit and one style source (ADOPT the lesson, AVOID the fragmentation).

## 12.13 WC.13 Loading, error and empty states — PARTIAL

| ID | Rule / value | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| WC.13.r01 | Use `ProgressBar` (determinate or indeterminate) or `ProgressRing`. ProgressRing can be scaled up but not below 20×20 epx. Use determinate when the duration is known. An indeterminate ProgressBar does not block interaction. An indeterminate ProgressRing signals that the user must wait (blocking). Both use `IsIndeterminate`. Paused/error properties were not captured in this extraction. | T1 [Progress controls (2026-07-08)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/develop/ui/controls/progress-controls) |
| WC.13.t01 | `ProgressBarMinHeight` `3` (WC.T2486); `ProgressBarTrackHeight` `1` (WC.T2487); `ProgressRingStrokeThickness` `4` (WC.T2496) | T2 [ProgressBar_themeresources.xaml](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/ProgressBar/ProgressBar_themeresources.xaml); [ProgressRing_themeresources.xaml](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/ProgressRing/ProgressRing_themeresources.xaml) |
| WC.13.r02 | `InfoBar` shows app-wide status inline and persistently. Severity is Informational, Success, Warning or Error, coloured by `SystemFillColor*` (§12.2.3). The bar is closable unless `IsClosable=false`, can hold an action button, and supports custom icons at 20 px. Users can close it with the close button, or it can be closed programmatically. For transient teaching moments a TeachingTip is the better option. `ActionButton` accepts a Button or HyperlinkButton. | T1 [InfoBar (2026-02-25)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/develop/ui/controls/infobar) |
| WC.13.t02 | `InfoBarMinHeight` `48` (WC.T1688); `InfoBarIconFontSize` `16` (WC.T1699) | T2 [InfoBar_themeresources.xaml](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/InfoBar/InfoBar_themeresources.xaml) |
| WC.13.r03 | `ContentDialog`: one per window at a time; at least one button; up to three button types (Primary, Secondary, Close), with the safe/nondestructive action always present. Size limits are `320` (WC.T1313)–`548` (WC.T1314) wide and `184` (WC.T1315)–`756` (WC.T1316) high (MEASURED). | T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/controls/dialogs-and-flyouts/dialogs); T2 [ContentDialog_themeresources.xaml](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-ui-xaml/blob/f980ab26e378db4afe4264c4f00c5bf6cc11ec34/controls/dev/CommonStyles/ContentDialog_themeresources.xaml) |
| WC.13.r04 | Status colour tokens: Success `#6CCB5F` / `#0F7B0F`, Caution `#FCE100` / `#9D5D00`, Critical `#FF99A4` / `#C42B1C` (Dark / Light), each with a matching background tint. | T2 §12.2.3 |
| WC.13.r05 | No official WinUI "empty state" pattern page was found on Microsoft Learn in this pass. Recorded as NOT FOUND within this leaf. | search of learn.microsoft.com, 2026-10-25 |

## 12.14 Docs-versus-code contradictions and open gaps

| ID | Topic | Source A | Source B | Assessment |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GAP-B3-01 | Segoe Fluent Icons glyph count | "more than 1,000 icons" ([Icons, 2024](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/style/icons)) | 1,574 unique code points in the official table (counted); 1,533 per [fluenticons.co](https://fluenticons.co/segoe-fluent-icons/) | The 2024 page is stale. The independent count differs by 41, possibly due to version or deduplication. Resolve by enumerating the font's cmap on a 26H2 machine (VP-09). |
| GAP-B3-02 | Exit easing | Exit = cubic-bezier(1,0,1,1) ([Timing and easing](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/motion/timing-and-easing)) | "Direct Exit" = (0,0,0,1); only "Gentle Exit" uses (1,0,1,1) ([Motion](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/signature-experiences/motion)) | Both pages are dated 2026-02-28. Source code defines only `ControlFastOutSlowInKeySpline` (0,0,0,1). No exit spline token exists. Treat (0,0,0,1) + fade as default and (1,0,1,1) as "gentle". |
| GAP-B3-03 | Durations | Tokens 83/167/250 ms (source); 167/250/333 ms (Motion page) | 300 / 150 ms examples ([Motion in practice, 2024](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/motion/motion-in-practice)) | The 2024 page reflects Windows 10-era timings. Use the token ladder. |
| GAP-B3-04 | Type ramp line heights and names | The docs give line heights 16…92 and labels like "Small/Text/Display" | Source sets FontSize only, with `LineStackingStrategy=MaxHeight` and keys like Caption/Body | Not a value conflict, but line heights are not encoded anywhere in source. |
| GAP-B3-05 | HighContrast colour sentinels | 83/83 HighContrast `Color` values = `#FF0000` (source) | No documentation of this pattern | Interpreted as a sentinel. Needs confirmation through a WinUI issue or design note. |
| GAP-B3-06 | `*_perf2026.xaml` variants | Present in source (Button, Expander) | No documentation, release note or commit message found; the commit history shows only a mirror sync (PR 15716130, 2026-05-30) | INVESTIGATE: which runtime path loads them, and whether they become the default. |
| GAP-B3-07 | Mica Alt / backdrop minimum build | "Windows 11 version 22000 or later" ([Mica](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/style/mica)) | `DWMWA_SYSTEMBACKDROP_TYPE` supported from 22621 ([DWM attribute reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/dwmapi/ne-dwmapi-dwmwindowattribute)) | These are different APIs (WinAppSDK controller vs DWM attribute), so this is not a direct contradiction. Note the different minimums for Win32. |
| GAP-B3-08 | Shadow recipe | Elevation depths only (docs) | No blur, offset or opacity is published, and ThemeShadow is implemented in closed code | Needs live measurement (VP-08). |
| GAP-B3-09 | Button min width | 120 px guidance ([Buttons](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/controls/buttons)) | No `MinWidth` in the default style (source) | Guidance is not enforced in code. |
| GAP-B3-10 | Shell vs WinUI | WinUI tokens (source) | Shell surfaces (Start, taskbar) are not built from this repo | Do not assume shell values equal WinUI values. Compare in VP-07. |

## 12.15 LitricOS implications (preliminary; final in Chapter 40, Research I)

| ID | Recommendation | Class | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| WC.I01 | A three-theme token architecture (light, dark, high contrast). The high-contrast theme maps every semantic role to a small set of user-chosen system colours (eight roles in WinUI). | ADOPT (concept) | §12.2.2 |
| WC.I02 | Semantic, alpha-based fill tokens (Primary/Secondary/Tertiary/Disabled) that compose on top of materials | ADAPT | §12.2.3 |
| WC.I03 | A two-radius geometry system (control and overlay) with 0 radius when maximized or snapped | ADAPT (choose own values) | §12.5 |
| WC.I04 | A frame-aligned duration ladder (≈83/167/250/333 ms) plus a single "fast-out, slow-in" default curve | ADAPT | §12.9 |
| WC.I05 | A material stack: an opaque wallpaper-tinted base for windows, plus a blurred translucent material for transient surfaces, with documented fallbacks (battery saver, transparency off, low GPU) | ADAPT | §12.7 |
| WC.I06 | A two-tone focus ring, shown for keyboard input only | ADOPT (concept) | §12.11 |
| WC.I07 | Multiple concurrent visual generations (WinUI, Win32 visual styles, uxguide dialogs) | AVOID | §12.12 |
| WC.I08 | Undocumented style forks (`perf2026`) | INVESTIGATE | GAP-B3-06 |
| WC.I09 | Copying Segoe fonts or icons, Fluent visual identity, or Microsoft colour values verbatim | AVOID | Rule 10 |

## Appendix 4A — Extracted rules and explicit values per source

Rows are listed verbatim from the structured extraction of each fully read page. IDs: `WC.xx.Rnnn` for rules and `WC.xx.Vnnn` for values, numbered per leaf. Values are DOCUMENTED (T1) or as stated by the independent source (T4/T5). Pages marked irrelevant or failed are listed in the register only.


### WC.01 Design principles


#### Windows APP Design

T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/) · updated: — · 11 rules, 0 values

| ID | Kind | Rule / claim |
|---|---|---|
| WC.01.R001 | do | Create Windows app experiences that are clear, accessible, responsive, and consistent with Fluent Design. |
| WC.01.R002 | do | Establish your design direction, explore the Windows guidelines, and include accessibility from the beginning. |
| WC.01.R003 | do | Use the principles that shape how Windows apps should look, feel, and behave. |
| WC.01.R004 | note | Find guidance for color, commanding, layout, materials, motion, navigation, typography, and usability. |
| WC.01.R005 | do | Plan for keyboard access, assistive technologies, adaptable visuals, and inclusive interaction patterns. |
| WC.01.R006 | do | Structure your app, adapt it to different window sizes, and help people move between pages and features. |
| WC.01.R007 | do | Build a coherent visual system by applying Windows color, material, elevation, and type guidance. |
| WC.01.R008 | do | Choose Windows iconography and create app assets that remain clear across sizes, themes, and surfaces. |
| WC.01.R009 | do | Use motion, haptics, and commanding patterns to communicate changes and support direct interaction. |
| WC.01.R010 | do | Make your app understandable and adaptable across abilities, languages, input methods, and preferences. |
| WC.01.R011 | do | Use Windows design kits, fonts, icons, templates, and related resources when creating and prototyping your app. |

#### learn.microsoft.com › en-us › windowsDesign principles - Windows apps \| Microsoft Learn

| WC.01.R012 | note | Windows 11 marks a visual evolution of the operating system. We have evolved our design language alongside of Fluent to create a design that is human, universal, and truly feels like Windows. |
| WC.01.R013 | do | Windows 11 is faster and more intuitive. It's easy to do what I want, with focus and precision. |
| WC.01.R014 | do | Windows 11 is softer and decluttered; it fades into the background to help me stay calm and focused. The experience feels warm, ethereal, and approachable. |
| WC.01.R015 | do | Windows 11 adapts seamlessly to the way I use my device. It bends and flexes to my individual needs and preferences so that I can truly express myself. |
| WC.01.R016 | do | Windows 11 balances a new, refreshed look and feel with the familiarity of the Windows I already know. There is no learning curve; I can pick it up and go. |
| WC.01.R017 | do | Windows 11 offers a visually seamless experience across platforms. I can work in many platforms and still have a consistent Windows experience. |
| WC.01.R018 | definition | Signature experiences are the design elements Windows 11 uses to express its visual language, while maintaining a coherent look and feel across all Fluent experiences. |
| WC.01.R019 | behavior | Color helps users focus on their tasks by indicating a visual hierarchy and structure between user interface elements. Windows 11 uses color to provide a calming foundation, subtly enhancing user interactions and emphasizing significant items only when necessary. |
| WC.01.R020 | definition | Elevation and layering is the concept of overlapping one surface with another, creating two or more visually distinguished areas within the same surface. Windows 11 uses elevation and layering as its foundation for app hierarchy. |
| WC.01.R021 | definition | Iconography is a set of visual images and symbols that help users understand and navigate your app. Windows 11 iconography has evolved in concert with our design language. Every glyph in our system icon font has been redesigned to embrace a softer geometry and more modern metaphors. |
| WC.01.R022 | definition | Materials are visual effects that make UI surfaces resemble real life artifacts. Windows 11 uses materials to keep the UI connected to its environment. Materials bring surfaces to life and help us distinguish between focused and unfocused applications. |
| WC.01.R023 | definition | Geometry describes the shape, size, and position of UI elements on screen. These fundamental design elements help experiences feel coherent across the entire design system. Windows 11 features updated geometry that creates a more approachable, engaging, and modern experience. |
| WC.01.R024 | behavior | As the visual representation of language, the main task of typography is to communicate information. The Windows 11 type system helps you create structure and hierarchy in your content in order to maximize legibility and readability in your UI. |
| WC.01.R025 | behavior | Motion describes the way the interface animates and responds to user interaction. Motion in Windows is reactive, direct, and context appropriate. It provides feedback to user input and reinforces spatial paradigms that support way-finding. |

#### Design tokens - Fluent 2 Design System

| ID | Kind | Rule / claim |
|---|---|---|
| WC.02.R061 | definition | Design tokens are stored values used to assign Fluent styles like color, typography, spacing, or elevation, without hardcoding pixels and hex codes. |
| WC.02.R062 | claim | Our token system allows us to speak a common language and ensure consistent designs across platforms and disciplines. |
| WC.02.R063 | definition | Fluent uses two layers of tokens. |
| WC.02.R064 | definition | Global tokens are context-agnostic and store raw values, like hex codes for color. |
| WC.02.R065 | definition | Global tokens also store information for typography, border radius, stroke width, and animation. |
| WC.02.R066 | definition | Alias tokens are Fluent’s second token layer and add semantic meaning to the stored values. |
| WC.02.R067 | claim | For more complex elements, like shadows and type, alias tokens condense many values into one easily digestible format. |
| WC.02.R068 | claim | We name alias tokens so that their function is immediately recognizable. |
| WC.02.R069 | claim | At a glance, you’ll get an idea of where and how to apply consistent styles without worrying about the exact values. |
| WC.02.R070 | claim | Our tokens support OS theming for light, dark, high-contrast, and branded elements. |
| WC.02.R071 | claim | Our tokens ensure sufficient color contrast across the system. |

#### Accessible text requirements - Windows apps

T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/accessibility/accessible-text-requirements) · updated: 08/26/2026 · 32 rules, 5 values

| ID | Kind | Rule / claim |
|---|---|---|
| WC.02.R072 | dont | Do not treat high-contrast mode as the primary mitigation for low readability. |
| WC.02.R073 | do | Base text design on sufficient foreground/background contrast in the default experience. |
| WC.02.R074 | claim | Contrast evaluation is deterministic and does not account for hue perception. |
| WC.02.R075 | do | Visible text must have a minimum luminance contrast ratio of 4.5:1 against its background. |
| WC.02.R076 | definition | Exceptions include logos and incidental text, such as text in inactive UI. |
| WC.02.R077 | definition | Decorative text that conveys no information is excluded. |
| WC.02.R078 | do | Use color contrast tools to verify that the visible text contrast ratio is acceptable. |
| WC.02.R079 | definition | TextBlock has the Text role; TextBox has the Edit role; RichTextBlock and RichTextBlockOverflow have the Text role; RichEditBox has the Edit role. |
| WC.02.R080 | behavior | When a control reports the Edit role, assistive technologies assume the value can be edited by the user. |
| WC.02.R081 | do | For static text, use TextBlock and RichTextBlock. |
| WC.02.R082 | dont | Avoid placing static text in focusable containers just to expose it through tab navigation. |
| WC.02.R083 | claim | If you use XAML AutosuggestBox or intrinsic HTML controls, most accessibility wiring is built in. |
| WC.02.R084 | do | To make custom implementations accessible, the input and suggestion list must be associated in the UIA tree. |
| WC.02.R085 | behavior | When the input and list are connected correctly, users can know when the list is present or closes, how many suggestions are available, and which item is selected, if any; they can move Narrator focus to the list and navigate through suggestions with other reading modes. |
| WC.02.R086 | do | Associate the input field and suggestion list in the UIA tree. |
| WC.02.R087 | do | For a default selection, fire the corresponding selected event whenever selection changes, including updates caused by typing or list navigation. |
| WC.02.R088 | do | If there is no default selection, raise the layout-invalidated event whenever the list updates. |
| WC.02.R089 | claim | With the default AutosuggestBox, required accessibility behavior is already wired. |
| WC.02.R090 | do | For custom XAML implementations, set AutomationProperties.ControlledPeers on the TextBox. Fire AutomationPropertyChanged whenever ControlledPeers is added or removed, and raise SelectionItemPatternOnElementSelected or LayoutInvalidated, based on your scenario. |
| WC.02.R091 | claim | With intrinsic HTML controls, the UIA mapping is already provided. |
| WC.02.R092 | do | If you build custom HTML controls, implement the required ARIA semantics as defined by W3C standards. |
| WC.02.R093 | dont | Avoid embedding text in graphics when possible. |
| WC.02.R094 | claim | Text rendered inside an Image source is not automatically readable by assistive technologies. |
| WC.02.R095 | do | If text in graphics is required, set AutomationProperties.Name to equivalent content or a concise semantic summary. |
| WC.02.R096 | do | Start with a reasonable default size throughout the app. |
| WC.02.R097 | do | Validate text against Windows text-related accessibility settings, including Magnifier, global scale and resolution settings, and text size settings. |
| WC.02.R098 | behavior | IsTextScaleFactorEnabled defaults to true; when enabled, text scales automatically, with smaller FontSize values typically affected more than larger ones. |
| WC.02.R099 | do | Set IsTextScaleFactorEnabled to false only when necessary. |
| WC.02.R100 | dont | Avoid disabling text scaling broadly. Consistent cross-app text scaling is an important accessibility capability. |
| WC.02.R101 | claim | WinUI text controls support the full text scaling experience without any customization or templating. |
| WC.02.R102 | do | For other WinRT-based apps, you can monitor TextScaleFactorChanged and get TextScaleFactor to react to system text size changes. |
| WC.02.R103 | dont | Do not assume uniform scaling across all text sizes. Larger text is generally affected less than smaller text. |

| WC.02.R111 | claim | This repo contains the full solution and source code for a tool that demonstrates the flexibility of the Fluent Design System and generates XAML markup for the ResourceDictionary framework used in UWP applications. |
| WC.02.R112 | claim | The source code was created with the Universal Windows Platform available in Visual Studio and is designed to run on desktop, mobile and future devices that support UWP. |
| WC.02.R113 | behavior | This application requires Visual Studio 2017 Update 4 or higher and Windows SDK version 17763 or higher for Windows 10. |
| WC.02.R114 | definition | Region is the background that all controls sit on; it is a separate resource that does not exist in the framework. |
| WC.02.R115 | definition | Base represents controls’ backplates and temporary state visuals such as hover or press. |
| WC.02.R116 | do | In general, Base should contrast with the theme background (Region color), and with black text in Light theme or white text in Dark theme. |
| WC.02.R117 | definition | Primary is essentially the Accent color and should contrast mainly with white text. |
| WC.02.R118 | behavior | Primary is also used in alternate rest states for toggled controls, such as list selection, checked checkboxes or radio buttons, slider fill values, and other control parts that differ from their default rest state after interaction. |
| WC.02.R119 | do | Click the chevron next to a major color swatch to access its detailed view of minor colors. |
| WC.02.R120 | definition | The presets dropdown is at the top of the Color Dictionary properties panel. |
| WC.02.R121 | behavior | On first boot, the preset is set to Default. |
| WC.02.R122 | behavior | Editing preset colors changes the preset selection to “Custom”; changes do not affect the other presets. |
| WC.02.R123 | do | Click Save to save a theme, or Load to open a saved JSON theme. |
| WC.02.R124 | behavior | The contrast information shows contrast with the most prevalent text color in the theme being edited. |
| WC.02.R125 | behavior | A color below the standard contrast ratio of 4.5:1 is flagged with red text next to its contrast value. |
| WC.02.R126 | do | Click Export at the bottom of the Color Dictionary properties panel to open a ResourceDictionary stub. |
| WC.02.R127 | behavior | The Export window refreshes with changed color values when the theme is changed and re-exported. |
| WC.02.R128 | do | Set the page background to the theme’s RegionBrush; it is the only brush that is not set automatically. |
| WC.02.R129 | claim | ColorPaletteResources allows SystemColors to be scoped at any level. |
| WC.02.R130 | behavior | Runtime color changes require reloading a ResourceDictionary for its resources to receive the new colors. |
| WC.02.R131 | behavior | The example reloads resources by changing RequestedTheme to a different theme and back again. |
| WC.02.R132 | claim | Changing colors through ColorPaletteResources at runtime too often may cause performance issues; the documentation says it should be acceptable in settings pages or places where users do not toggle it often. |
| WC.02.R133 | claim | The exported ColorPaletteResources API requires version 17744 or greater. |
| WC.02.R134 | do | For earlier SDK versions, define each SystemColor individually with colors from the theme to enable the theme across controls. |
| WC.02.R135 | claim | The downlevel markup format works only at the app.xaml level for earlier SDKs; it does not work at page-, container-, or control-scoped level. |
| WC.02.R136 | claim | The project welcomes input and suggestions but is not accepting public features or changes at this time. |

#### abacktools.com › color-tools › windows-11-colorsWindows 11 Accent Colors Reference \| Aback Tools

T4 [abacktools.com](https://abacktools.com/tools/design/color-tools/windows-11-colors) · updated: October 2026 · 20 rules, 102 values

| ID | Kind | Rule / claim |
|---|---|---|
| WC.02.R137 | claim | The 17 accent entries in this reference are sample accent colours with descriptive names, not the official Settings swatch list. |
| WC.02.R138 | behavior | When a user selects an accent colour, it becomes the SystemAccentColor and dynamically propagates through all WinUI controls. |
| WC.02.R139 | definition | Includes the base colour plus three lighter variants (Light1, Light2, Light3) and three darker variants (Dark1, Dark2, Dark3). |
| WC.02.R140 | behavior | Windows calculates the real variants from the accent the user picks. |
| WC.02.R141 | behavior | If the user changes their accent colour, these tokens update automatically throughout the UI. |
| WC.02.R142 | claim | The background, text and status values here were checked against the WinUI 3 theme resources in October 2026; the control, material, link and divider values are approximations. |
| WC.02.R143 | claim | Microsoft can change these between Windows releases. |
| WC.02.R144 | bug | WinUI spells this key "Quarternary". |
| WC.02.R145 | behavior | Semi-transparent text colours are shown composited on white or black. |
| WC.02.R146 | behavior | Card Background (Light) is WinUI #B3FFFFFF (70% white) - shown opaque. |
| WC.02.R147 | definition | Mica is a desktop-tinted material that samples the wallpaper colour to create a subtle, static tint on window backgrounds. |
| WC.02.R148 | definition | Acrylic is a semi-transparent blur material used in flyouts, context menus, and the Settings app. |
| WC.02.R149 | claim | The full visual effect depends on the desktop wallpaper and the blur/opacity parameters applied by the OS. |
| WC.02.R150 | definition | WinUI defines status fill colours for conveying status: Critical (red), Success (green) and Caution (amber). |
| WC.02.R151 | definition | Each includes a foreground colour and a paired background fill. |
| WC.02.R152 | do | Use the theme resources themselves rather than copied HEX values: in XAML, reference them as {ThemeResource SystemAccentColor} or {ThemeResource SolidBackgroundFillColorBase} so they follow the theme and accent. |
| WC.02.R153 | claim | Some control, material and link entries use descriptive names that are not built-in keys, and their values are approximations. |
| WC.02.R154 | claim | Microsoft can adjust the WinUI theme resources between Windows and WinUI releases. |
| WC.02.R155 | do | Always test your design on the specific OS version you are targeting. |
| WC.02.R156 | claim | The reference includes separate entries for light-theme and dark-theme variants of text, icons, backgrounds, acrylic, mica, layers, and dividers. |

### WC.03 Typography ramp (Segoe UI Variable)


#### Typography in Windows - Windows apps

T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/signature-experiences/typography) · updated: 04/14/2026 · 26 rules, 21 values

| ID | Kind | Rule / claim |
|---|---|---|
| WC.03.R001 | definition | The Windows type system helps you create structure and hierarchy in your content in order to maximize legibility and readability in your UI. |
| WC.03.R002 | do | Use one font throughout your app's UI, and we recommend sticking with the default font for Windows apps, Segoe UI Variable. |
| WC.03.R003 | do | Use regular weight for most text, use Semibold for titles |
| WC.03.R004 | do | Align left by default, Align center only in rare cases such as text below icons |
| WC.03.R005 | do | Use sentence casing for all UI text, including titles |
| WC.03.R006 | do | Use ellipses in most cases; clipping is only used in rare cases |
| WC.03.R007 | behavior | Font sizes in XAML apps automatically scale on all devices. |
| WC.03.R008 | do | Because of how the scaling system works, you're designing in effective pixels, not actual physical pixels, and you shouldn't have to alter font sizes for different screens sizes or resolutions. |
| WC.03.R009 | do | To create a clear visual hierarchy in your app, follow the Windows type ramp. |
| WC.03.R010 | note | Bold and Italic styles are not part of the Windows type ramp. Use Semibold instead of Bold for emphasis. Italic is excluded because it can reduce readability and legibility, particularly for people with dyslexia. |
| WC.03.R011 | do | The default TextAlignment is Left, and in most instances, flush-left and ragged right provides consistent anchoring of the content and a uniform layout. |
| WC.03.R012 | do | Keep to 50–60 letters per line for ease of reading. |
| WC.03.R013 | dont | Don't use fewer than 20 characters or more than 60 characters per line as this is difficult to read. |
| WC.03.R014 | do | When the amount of text extends beyond the space available, we recommend clipping the text and inserting ellipses [...], which is the default behavior of most WinUI text controls. |
| WC.03.R015 | do | Clip text, and wrap if multiple lines are enabled. |
| WC.03.R016 | dont | Don't use ellipses to avoid visual clutter. |
| WC.03.R017 | do | If containers are not well-defined (for example, no differentiating background color), or when there is a link to see more text, then use ellipses. |
| WC.03.R018 | do | Use the LanguageFont font-mapping APIs for programmatic access to the recommended font family, size, weight, and style for a particular language. |
| WC.03.R019 | do | Sans-serif fonts are a great choice for headings and UI elements. |
| WC.03.R020 | do | Serif fonts are good for presenting large amounts of text. |
| WC.03.R021 | do | Variable fonts are good for precisely controlling the appearance of text. |
| WC.03.R022 | definition | The weight axis (`wght`) is incremental with weights from Thin (100) to Bold (700). |
| WC.03.R023 | behavior | The optical size axis (`opsz`) is automatic and on by default. |
| WC.03.R024 | behavior | When using XAML common controls, the Segoe UI Variable font will be selected by default for supported languages. |
| WC.03.R025 | behavior | When this font or another variable font with an optical axis is used, the optical size will automatically match the requested font-size. |
| WC.03.R026 | do | When using HTML, optical scaling is also automatic, but you will need to specify the Segoe UI Variable font in CSS. |

#### How to use Segoe UI Variable in a website \| Tiger Oakes

T4 [tigeroakes.com](https://tigeroakes.com/posts/segoe-ui-variable/) · updated: 2022, May 25 · 9 rules, 17 values

| ID | Kind | Rule / claim |
|---|---|---|
| WC.03.R047 | claim | Windows 11 introduced Segoe UI Variable, a variable font that replaces the older Segoe UI font in Windows 10 and below. |
| WC.03.R048 | claim | Setting `font-family: 'Segoe UI Variable'` does not work in browsers as of May 2022; the browser falls back to another font. |
| WC.03.R049 | do | Use one of these names to access the local font: 'Segoe UI Variable Text', 'Segoe UI Variable Small', or 'Segoe UI Variable Display'. |
| WC.03.R050 | definition | Segoe UI Variable has two axes of variation: font weight and optical size. |
| WC.03.R051 | behavior | Optical size corresponds roughly to the CSS `font-size` property and changes the font’s shape depending on text size. |
| WC.03.R052 | claim | The version of Segoe UI Variable that comes with Windows 11 does not have optical size variation, or it does not work in web browsers. |
| WC.03.R053 | do | The downloadable version has all three optical size axes bundled into one file; use `font-variation-settings` to specify optical size. |
| WC.03.R054 | do | The article says to place `font-weight` and `font-style` after `font-variation-settings`, otherwise they will be overridden by it. |
| WC.03.R055 | claim | Segoe UI is copyrighted by Microsoft; the article says to use the downloadable font only when working on a Microsoft site. |

### WC.04 Spacing & layout grid


#### Compact sizing for controls - Windows apps \| Microsoft Learn

T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/develop/ui/controls/compact-sizing) · updated: 2026-07-07 · 13 rules, 0 values

| ID | Kind | Rule / claim |
|---|---|---|
| WC.04.R001 | definition | WinUI 3 controls are designed with spacing optimized for touch interaction. |
| WC.04.R002 | do | For desktop applications where users primarily use a mouse or keyboard, you can apply a compact density style to reduce the height and padding of controls. |
| WC.04.R003 | behavior | This makes more content visible on screen without requiring the user to scroll. |
| WC.04.R004 | behavior | This override applies to all controls within the page. |
| WC.04.R005 | do | You can scope it to a specific panel by placing the resource dictionary in the panel's Resources instead of the page's Resources. |
| WC.04.R006 | do | To apply compact sizing to all pages in your application, add the resource dictionary to App.xaml. |
| WC.04.R007 | do | Merge the compact dictionary after XamlControlsResources so that it correctly overrides the default size values. |
| WC.04.R008 | do | You can toggle compact sizing at runtime by adding or removing the resource dictionary from a panel's resources. |
| WC.04.R009 | do | Productivity or data-entry apps (tables, forms, settings): Compact sizing |
| WC.04.R010 | do | Consumer apps or apps designed for touch input: Default sizing |
| WC.04.R011 | do | Mixed apps with a settings panel: Apply compact sizing scoped to the settings panel only |
| WC.04.R012 | definition | WinUI 3 provides only two density levels: default and compact. |
| WC.04.R013 | note | There is no API to set an arbitrary density value. |

| WC.04.R017 | do | When differentiating a title in a confined UI space, use Body Strong for the title without any additional spacing between text blocks. |

#### Why are TVs considered "small"?

T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/layout/screen-sizes-and-breakpoints-for-responsive-design) · updated: 07/13/2026 · 8 rules, 39 values

| ID | Kind | Rule / claim |
|---|---|---|
| WC.04.R035 | do | Rather than optimizing your UI for every screen size, design for a few key width categories called breakpoints: |
| WC.04.R036 | do | When you design for specific breakpoints, design for the amount of screen space available to your app (the app's window), not the physical screen size. |
| WC.04.R037 | definition | When you specify a size for a control or a breakpoint range, you use effective pixels. |
| WC.04.R038 | definition | When you design your XAML app, you design in effective pixels, not actual physical pixels. |
| WC.04.R039 | do | You can ignore pixel density and the actual screen resolution when you design. Instead, design for the effective resolution (the resolution in effective pixels) for a size class. |
| WC.04.R040 | do | When you create screen mockups in image editing programs, set the DPI to 72 and set the image dimensions to the effective resolution for the size class you're targeting. |
| WC.04.R041 | do | The sizes, margins, and positions of UI elements should always be in multiples of 4 epx in your XAML apps. |
| WC.04.R042 | note | Text does not have this requirement; text can have any size and position. |

### WC.05 Geometry & corner radius


#### Geometry in Windows 11 - Windows apps \| Microsoft Learn

T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/signature-experiences/geometry) · updated: 02/19/2026 (shown for the related Training module; no article update date is shown) · 16 rules, 3 values

| ID | Kind | Rule / claim |
|---|---|---|
| WC.05.R001 | definition | Geometry describes the shape, size and position of UI elements on screen. |
| WC.05.R002 | behavior | Windows 11 applies rounded corners to all top-level app windows. |
| WC.05.R003 | behavior | The same applies to most common controls such as Button and ListView. |
| WC.05.R004 | do | Top-level containers such as app windows, flyouts and dialogs are rounded using an 8px corner radius. |
| WC.05.R005 | do | In-page elements such as buttons and list backplates are rounded using a 4px corner radius. |
| WC.05.R006 | dont | Straight edges that intersect with other straight edges are not rounded. |
| WC.05.R007 | dont | Window corners are not rounded when windows are snapped or maximized. |
| WC.05.R008 | do | Standard controls that users see on screen at all times use a 4px corner radius. |
| WC.05.R009 | do | Transient UI elements that appear temporarily on screen, or overlay other UI, use an 8px corner radius. |
| WC.05.R010 | note | ToolTip is an exception, using 4px due to its small size. |
| WC.05.R011 | do | UI elements shaped like bars or lines use a 4px corner radius. |
| WC.05.R012 | dont | When multiple UI elements that are housed inside a container touch each other, such as the two parts of a SplitButton. There should be no space when they contact. |
| WC.05.R013 | dont | When a flyout UI element is connected to a UI that invokes the flyout on one side. |
| WC.05.R014 | behavior | The default corner radii are controlled by two global resources: ControlCornerRadius (default 4px) and OverlayCornerRadius (default 8px). |
| WC.05.R015 | do | You can override these values in your App.xaml to change the rounding across all controls in your app. |
| WC.05.R016 | do | If you're building a Win32 desktop app, you can opt in to rounded corners for your app windows using the DWM API. |

| ID | Name | Value | Context |
|---|---|---|---|
| WC.05.V001 | 8px | 8px | Corner radius for top-level containers such as app windows, flyouts and dialogs; also for transient UI elements that appear temporarily or overlay other UI. |
| WC.05.V002 | 4px | 4px | Corner radius for in-page elements, standard controls, bar or line elements, and ToolTip. |
| WC.05.V003 | 0px | 0px | Straight edges that intersect with other straight edges are not rounded; window corners are also not rounded when windows are snapped or maximized. |

#### Apply rounded corners in desktop apps - Windows apps

T1 [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/desktop/modernize/ui/apply-rounded-corners) · updated: 07/07/2026 · 18 rules, 9 values

| ID | Kind | Rule / claim |
|---|---|---|
| WC.05.R017 | behavior | On Windows 11, the system automatically rounds top-level window corners for all inbox apps, including all WinUI apps, and most other apps. |
| WC.05.R018 | behavior | By design, apps are not rounded when maximized, snapped, running in a Virtual Machine (VM), running in an Azure Virtual Desktop (AVD) session, or running as a Windows Defender Application Guard (WDAG) window. |
| WC.05.R019 | behavior | Apps that want a complete system-provided frame and caption-controls, or provide enough information to the system to round them, are rounded by default. |
| WC.05.R020 | behavior | Lack of frame styles, an empty non-client area, and other customizations such as extra non-child windows used for custom shadows can prevent automatic rounding. |
| WC.05.R021 | claim | The opt-in API is a hint to the system and does not guarantee rounding; the result depends on the customizations. |
| WC.05.R022 | behavior | Apps with per-pixel alpha layering or window regions cannot ever be rounded, even if they call the opt-in API. |
| WC.05.R023 | definition | DWMWCP_DEFAULT lets the system decide whether or not to round window corners. |
| WC.05.R024 | definition | DWMWCP_DONOTROUND never rounds window corners. |
| WC.05.R025 | definition | DWMWCP_ROUND rounds the corners if appropriate. |
| WC.05.R026 | definition | DWMWCP_ROUNDSMALL rounds the corners if appropriate, with a small radius. |
| WC.05.R027 | do | Pass a pointer to the desired DWM_WINDOW_CORNER_PREFERENCE value as the third parameter of DwmSetWindowAttribute. |
| WC.05.R028 | do | Pass DWMWA_WINDOW_CORNER_PREFERENCE as the second parameter of DwmSetWindowAttribute. |
| WC.05.R029 | behavior | All standard WinForms and WPF apps are rounded automatically, but custom window frames or third-party frameworks might require opting in. |
| WC.05.R030 | do | For WPF, use EnsureHandle to force creation of an HWND before the window is shown. |
| WC.05.R031 | do | For WinForms, the Form's Handle property provides the HWND; call DwmSetWindowAttribute after InitializeComponent in the constructor. |
| WC.05.R032 | do | For a native C++ app, call DwmSetWindowAttribute in the message-processing function after window creation to ask the system to round the window. |
| WC.05.R033 | behavior | Pop-up windows such as menus do not get rounded by default. |
| WC.05.R034 | do | For a custom menu that should follow the rounding policy of standard menus, call the API to request rounding with the small radius because menus are auxiliary UI. |

| ID | Name | Value | Context |
|---|---|---|---|
| WC.05.V004 | Windows version | 11 | Windows 11 in the title and article text. |
| WC.05.V005 | Non-client area border | 1 pixel | A 1-pixel non-client area border can give the system enough information to round corners. |
| WC.05.V006 | DWMWA_WINDOW_CORNER_PREFERENCE | 33 | Value assigned to the DWMWINDOWATTRIBUTE enumeration in the C# examples. |
| WC.05.V007 | DWMWCP_DEFAULT | 0 | DWM_WINDOW_CORNER_PREFERENCE enum value; let the system decide whether to round. |
| WC.05.V008 | DWMWCP_DONOTROUND | 1 | DWM_WINDOW_CORNER_PREFERENCE enum value; never round. |
| WC.05.V009 | DWMWCP_ROUND | 2 | DWM_WINDOW_CORNER_PREFERENCE enum value; round if appropriate. |
| WC.05.V010 | DWMWCP_ROUNDSMALL | 3 | DWM_WINDOW_CORNER_PREFERENCE enum value; round if appropriate with a small radius. |

#### Windows 11でのウィンドウの形状とコントロールの形状 #2

T4 [nishy-software.com](https://nishy-software.com/ja/dev-sw/windows-11-ui-design-2/) · updated: 投稿日: 2021年8月30日（2021年8月29日） · 25 rules, 23 values

| ID | Kind | Rule / claim |
|---|---|---|
| WC.05.R060 | behavior | Windows 11では、ウィンドウやコントロールの四角が丸みを帯びます。 |
| WC.05.R061 | definition | トップレベルのコンテナ（アプリのウィンドウ、フライアウト、ダイアログなど）は、8pxのコーナー半径で丸められます。 |
| WC.05.R062 | definition | ページ内要素（ボタンやリストのバックプレートなど）は、4pxのコーナー半径で丸められます。 |
| WC.05.R063 | definition | 他の直線エッジと交差する直線エッジは丸められません。 |
| WC.05.R064 | behavior | ウィンドウをスナップしたり最大化したりすると、ウィンドウの角は丸められません。 |
| WC.05.R065 | definition | 8pxや4pxは、96DPIを基準（100%）とした論理ピクセルです。 |
| WC.05.R066 | behavior | 標準的な方法で作成されたデスクトップアプリでは、四角の丸みが多くの場合自動的に適用されます。 |
| WC.05.R067 | behavior | アプリはAPIを呼び出して、四角の丸みを明示的に適用することをシステムに宣言したり、自動適用から除外することを宣言したりできます。 |
| WC.05.R068 | do | 四角の丸みの適用・除外には、DwmSetWindowAttribute()を利用します。 |
| WC.05.R069 | behavior | DwmSetWindowAttribute()は、ウィンドウを作成した後にウィンドウの四角の丸みに関する設定をします。 |
| WC.05.R070 | do | dwAttributeにはDWMWINDOWATTRIBUTE列挙値のDWMWA_WINDOW_CORNER_PREFERENCEを指定します。 |
| WC.05.R071 | do | pvAttributeのバッファには、DWM_WINDOW_CORNER_PREFERENCE列挙値のいずれかを設定します。 |
| WC.05.R072 | definition | DWMWCP_DEFAULTは、システムにウィンドウの角を丸めるかどうかを判断させます。 |
| WC.05.R073 | definition | DWMWCP_DONOTROUNDは、ウィンドウの角を丸めません。 |
| WC.05.R074 | definition | DWMWCP_ROUNDは、適切な場合に角を丸めます。 |
| WC.05.R075 | definition | DWMWCP_ROUNDSMALLは、適切な場合に小さい半径で角を丸めます。 |
| WC.05.R076 | do | 丸みの適用を強制するにはDWMWCP_ROUNDを設定します。 |
| WC.05.R077 | dont | 丸みの適用を拒否するにはDWMWCP_DONOTROUNDを設定します。 |
| WC.05.R078 | behavior | Window Corner preferenceの設定は、設定したウィンドウハンドルにのみ有効で、子ウィンドウには影響しません。 |
| WC.05.R079 | claim | Win32ダイアログリソースでは、ボタンやチェックボックスなどのコントロールは子ウィンドウであり、HWNDを持ちます。 |
| WC.05.R080 | claim | WPFでは、ボタンやチェックボックスなどのページ内要素はWPFで描画され、ウィンドウではないためHWNDを持ちません。 |
| WC.05.R081 | behavior | WPFのページ内要素は、今回の設定の影響を受けません。 |
| WC.05.R082 | definition | DWMWCP_DEFAULTは、アプリが値を設定しないときの既定値です。 |
| WC.05.R083 | behavior | DWMWCP_DONOTROUNDでは、ウィンドウの角が丸められず、Windows 10と同じ形状になります。 |
| WC.05.R084 | claim | DWMWCP_ROUNDSMALLでは角が小さく丸められ、この設定はボタンなどダイアログ内のコントロールに対して使用すべき設定のようです。 |

| WC.06.R008 | do | Shadows help create the appearance of elevation, guiding the user's focus to the most important elements in your UI. |
| WC.06.R009 | note | Elevated objects in your UI don't need to have shadows, but they help create the appearance of elevation. |
| WC.06.R010 | do | In Windows apps, shadows should be used in a purposeful rather than aesthetic manner. |
| WC.06.R011 | dont | Using too many shadows will decrease or eliminate the ability of the shadow to focus the user. |
| WC.06.R012 | behavior | If you use standard controls, shadows are already incorporated into your UI. |
| WC.06.R013 | behavior | It applies shadows to elements based on z-depth value, emulating depth. |
| WC.06.R014 | behavior | It keeps shadows consistent throughout and across applications thanks to built in shadow aesthetics. |
| WC.06.R015 | behavior | ThemeShadow automatically casts shadows when applied to any XAML element in a Popup. |
| WC.06.R016 | do | For most Popup UI, the recommended default elevation relative to the app background content is 32 effective pixels. |
| WC.06.R017 | dont | Receivers cannot be an ancestor of the caster in the visual tree. |
| WC.06.R018 | behavior | Starting with Windows 11, if the app targets the Windows SDK version 22000 or later, the Receivers collection is ignored. |
| WC.06.R019 | behavior | DropShadow does not provide built in shadow values and you need to specify them yourself. |
| WC.06.R020 | behavior | Starting with Windows 11, if the app targets the Windows SDK version 22000 or later, ThemeShadow will behave like a drop shadow. |
| WC.06.R021 | do | Keep in mind that the purpose of shadow is to provide meaningful hierarchy, not as a simple visual treatment. |
| WC.06.R022 | do | Generally, we recommend using ThemeShadow, which provides consistent shadow values. |
| WC.06.R023 | do | For concerns about performance, limit the number of shadows, use other visual treatment, or use DropShadow. |
| WC.06.R024 | do | If you have more advanced scenarios to achieve visual hierarchy, consider using other visual treatment (for example, color). |
| WC.06.R025 | do | If shadow is needed, then use DropShadow. |

| WC.06.R048 | do | Generally, the article recommends using ThemeShadow, which provides consistent shadow values. |
| WC.06.R049 | do | For concerns about performance, limit the number of shadows, use other visual treatment, or use DropShadow. |
| WC.06.R050 | do | If advanced scenarios require visual hierarchy, consider other visual treatment (for example, color); if shadow is needed, use DropShadow. |
| WC.06.R051 | claim | ThemeShadow is adaptable and supports 3D environments, although its shadows are emulated; it is not customizable. DropShadow is customizable, not adaptable, and is not supported in 3D environments. |
